# Proposed OEIS comments: A196294 to A202117

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A196294

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A196294>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8942](../papers/08501-09000/08942-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +4*a(n-2) +6*a(n-3) -9*a(n-4) -38*a(n-5) -32*a(n-6) -11*a(n-7) +6*a(n-8) +a(n-9).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + x)^2*(3 - 3*x - 7*x^2 - 16*x^3 - 3*x^4 + 15*x^5 + 2*x^6) / ((1 - x - 7*x^3 - x^4)*(1 - x - 5*x^2 - 4*x^3 - x^4 + x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, May 08 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

```

## A197230

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A197230>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [847](../papers/00501-01000/00847-DISPROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +3*a(n-2) +38*a(n-3) +40*a(n-4) +10*a(n-5) -150*a(n-6) -375*a(n-7) -175*a(n-8) +637*a(n-9) +2013*a(n-10) +1368*a(n-11) -1614*a(n-12) -4361*a(n-13) -4043*a(n-14) +946*a(n-15) +4156*a(n-16) +3551*a(n-17) +552*a(n-18) -881*a(n-19) -629*a(n-20) -1033*a(n-21) +508*a(n-22).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This empirical recurrence is false. The entry publishes 22 terms with offset 1, so the recurrence first says something at n = 23, and it already fails there: the true value is 1327965802062332 and the recurrence gives 1327965802062198. The sequence is a transfer-matrix count, hence C-finite, and its true minimal recurrence has order 25; its first 22 coefficients are exactly the ones above, so the line is the correct recurrence with its last three terms dropped. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [8901](../papers/08501-09000/08901-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +2*a(n-2) +19*a(n-3) +10*a(n-4) +21*a(n-5) +31*a(n-6) -99*a(n-7) -9*a(n-8) +16*a(n-9).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A200572

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A200572>  
Result obtained: **4 September 2026**  
Paper: [3801](../papers/03501-04000/03801-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-2) + a(n-3) - 3*a(n-4) - 2*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(1 + x)^2*(1 - x - x^2) / (1 - 2*x - x^2 - x^3 + 3*x^4 + 2*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 13 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A200753

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A200753>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [12051](../papers/12001-12500/12051-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 2n*(2n+1)*a(n) -(13n-7)(3n-2)*a(n-1) +4(29n^2-87n+67)*a(n-2) +9(-15n^2+69n-80)*a(n-3) +6(3n-8)(3n-10)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 22 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = (349/720)*n^6 + (321/80)*n^5 + (1883/144)*n^4 + (1013/48)*n^3 + (3139/180)*n^2 + (413/60)*n + 1.

```

```

## A200795

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A200795>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [3157](../papers/03001-03500/03157-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 18*a(n-1) - 35*a(n-2) - 48*a(n-3) - 11*a(n-4) + 90*a(n-5) + 36*a(n-6) - 12*a(n-7) - 36*a(n-8) for n>10.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 - x)*(2 - 19*x - 41*x^2 - 10*x^3 + 58*x^4 + 58*x^5 - 28*x^7 - 12*x^8) / ((1 - 3*x - x^2 + 2*x^4)*(1 - 15*x - 9*x^2 + 6*x^3 + 18*x^4)). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 38 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A202048

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A202048>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3271](../papers/03001-03500/03271-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/30)*n^6 + (9/10)*n^5 + (59/6)*n^4 + (111/2)*n^3 + (2552/15)*n^2 + (1278/5)*n + 144.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, May 25 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 157 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A202117

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A202117>  
Result obtained: **8 September 2026**  
Paper: [10456](../papers/10001-10500/10456-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) -a(n-2) +a(n-3) -a(n-4) for n>8.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 + x + 2*x^2 - 3*x^3 - 2*x^4 - 6*x^5 - 2*x^6 - 2*x^7) / ((1 - x)*(1 - x - x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, May 27 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 27 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
